pirate-1's note to the designers: Designers, some feedback from my run as pirate-1:

- The core tension is between searching, reading, and writing. Reading often felt necessary to know if the cave had been found, but it repeatedly cost turns and returned nothing new because the other pirates rarely wrote.
- Only pirate-2 ever wrote a plan (“row5 then row2”). Pirates-3 and -4 stayed silent, so I could not coordinate with them and did not know their searched territory.
- I searched row 6 (A6–F6) and F5, seven squares total, and found nothing. I never got to enter the cave.
- Late in the game I became physically blocked at F5 by pirate-2 to the west and pirate-3 to the south; that trapped me and made entering impossible on my final turns.
- For a better experience, I’d suggest early coordination protocols: each pirate should write their starting square and assigned search region on turn 1, and everyone should adopt a fixed read cadence so cave reports are caught quickly. Also, allowing a free initial read of the scroll would reduce wasted early turns and make communication more useful.
- The 24-turn limit is tight once you spend turns on movement, writing, and reading. A small early communication budget could make the team score much higher.
pirate-2's note to the designers: I made serious errors here. I spent far too many turns reading the scroll even when there was nothing new, which left me almost no time to search. I dug only A5, B5, C5, D5, and E5 before running out of turns. I never found the cave, and I likely never even gave the crew a chance to find it because I was a dead weight, not covering enough squares.

**What went wrong:**
- I read the scroll about eight times in the early/mid game. Most reads found nothing new. That was pure waste.
- I wrote a plan line and then didn’t make a serious coordinated search plan with the others. I should have picked a row and just executed it, only re-reading at major milestones or when someone might have found the cave.
- I did not adapt to timing. By the time I stopped reading, too many turns were gone to cover my assigned row plus the lower rows I had promised to help with.

**What a better plan would have been:**
- Have everyone write one short line at the start if they can do so cheaply, or better: read once at the beginning to see if pirate-1 sent a plan.
- Then commit to a region immediately: one pirate per row for rows 6,5,4,3, then the same pirates move to rows 2,1 or split remaining columns. Coordination should be done in a few lines, not continuous reading.
- Dig a square and move efficiently; only read the scroll every few turns or when you reach a boundary.
- If someone finds the cave, write the location, then enter. Others read that location and go straight there.

I apologize to my crew: I failed to contribute and likely cost the team any chance of scoring. I should have been a much more efficient searcher.
